## Related
This builds upon previous memory UI enhancements and continues the effort to improve the ElizaOS client interface.88:Ta39,## Pull Request: Add Livepeer LLM Inference Plugin to Eliza Framework

### Description
This PR introduces the **Livepeer LLM Inference Plugin** for the Eliza framework. It enables language-model inference via Livepeer’s API gateway, allowing agents to leverage a variety of models without requiring individual API keys.

---

### Features
- **LLM Inference Processing**  
  Seamless POST requests to Livepeer’s `/llm` endpoint for chat-style interactions.
- **Multi-Model Support**  
  Automatically selects from small or large models, or custom endpoints, based on configuration.
- **Zero-Key Integration**  
  No need for proprietary API keys—uses gateway defaults or runtime settings.

---

### Implementation Details
- **New Package**  
  Added `packages/plugin-livepeer-inference/` containing the TypeScript plugin and Zod schema.
- **Config Schema**  
  Validates and parses:  
  - `LIVEPEER_GATEWAY_URL`  
  - Optional model overrides (`LIVEPEER_MODEL`, `LIVEPEER_LARGE_MODEL`)  
  - Runtime overrides for `temperature` and `maxTokens`
- **Core Logic**  
  - `callLivepeerLLM()` helper builds messages and handles HTTP calls  
  - Exposes three hooks under `ModelType`:  
    - `TEXT_SMALL`  
    - `TEXT_LARGE`  
    - `TEXT_EMBEDDING` (character-code embedding fallback)

---

### Setup & Usage
1. **Install**  
   ```bash
   pnpm install packages/plugin-livepeer-inference
   ```
2. **Configure**  
   - Add your gateway URL in `.env` or runtime settings:  
     ```env
     LIVEPEER_GATEWAY_URL=https://your-gateway.livepeer.com
     LIVEPEER_MODEL=meta-llama/Meta-Llama-3.1-8B-Instruct
     ```
   - (Optional) Override temperature or max tokens:  
     ```env
     LIVEPEER_TEMPERATURE=0.7
     LIVEPEER_MAX_TOKENS=1024
     ```
3. **Enable**  
   Include `"plugin-livepeer-inference"` in your agent’s `plugins` array.

---

### Testing
- **Test Suite**:  
  - Core inference flows covered under `packages/plugin-livepeer-inference/tests/*`  
  - Zod schema validation tests for missing/invalid config
- **Integration**:  
  Verified against [[UD1sto/elizav2-livepeer](https://github.com/UD1sto/elizav2-livepeer)](https://github.com/UD1sto/elizav2-livepeer)
- **Known Issue**:  
  - Intermittent `503 Gateway` errors on reflection LLM calls from the bootstrap plugin. Under investigation; core flow still completes successfully.

---

### Checklist
- [x] Added TypeScript typings and Zod schema  
- [x] Wrote unit tests covering main code paths  
- [x] Verified end-to-end flow in demo repository  
- [ ] Resolve 503 error

elizaos plugins list|l

Working – currently returning a hardcoded list. This should ideally return a list connected to elizaos-plugins, npm, etc. But we will leave this for now.

elizaos plugins add|install

Issues with dependency loops when installing plugins in monorepo context (e.g. plugins that are in packages dir). Can’t handle workspace dependencies properly or distinguish between monorepo root and other projects.

PROPOSED SOLUTION: Add monorepo context detection, Add workspace package detection, use special handling for monorepo root (uses workspace:* references in package.json). Avoids npm install for root to prevent dependency loops. For other projects in monorepo, falls back to standard installation. Added fallback mechanisms if workspace installation fails

elizaos plugins installed-plugins

Working – lists plugins that are referenced as deps in package.json, this is correct implementation for portable monorepo design

elizaos plugins installed-plugins

AGENT COMMANDS

elizaos agent -h/–help

Works correctly, displays helper text, options, and commands as expected

elizaos agent list|ls (-j/–json)

Works correctly – if you run it with no agents running it gives correct error, if its run with agents running it gives the correct agent info. JSON flag works too.

elizaos agent get|g (-n/–name, -j/–json, -o/–output)

A few minor bugs:

The --json flag incorrectly requires a name parameter when it should display all agents in JSON format without requiring any name parameter.
When using elizaos agent get -n eliza --json, the command incorrectly saves to a file named "eliza" instead of displaying the JSON output in the terminal.
The --output flag doesn't work at all. When using -n eliza --output testie, it should save the agent data as JSON to "testie.json" but currently does nothing.
elizaos agent get without a flag passed shows the same exact table as list. But get’s purpose should be about showing the char file in the terminal as opposed to list which just shows all the agents running with their names and ids and status.

PROPOSED SOLUTION: make --json display JSON output without saving to file, make --output save to file without displaying, and allowing --json to work without requiring a name parameter to list all agents. If user runs agent get without a flag, it shows an interactive menu showing all available agents.

elizaos agent start|s (-n/–name, –path)

Several issues, but broadly, it was confusing create and start functions, and had some options that didnt make sense. We would expect elizaos agent start -n ELIZA to start an instance of an existing agent character JSON called eliza in the current dir. Similar behavior would be expected with a –path passed. But this was failing with a "Bad Request" error. The command's scope was unclear - it was attempting to both create new agents and start existing ones, which made the error handling and response parsing complex and error-prone. Also it had the –json flag which didnt make sense to have at all. And the –remote-url is something that makes sense to pass for agent creation, not for agent starting.

PROPOSED SOLUTION: The solution was to clarify the command's scope to focus solely on starting existing agents, removing the outdated options from the help text. We implemented a consistent two-step process: first create the agent if it doesn't exist (using the character JSON), then start its runtime. This was implemented uniformly across all three ways to use the command: by name (-n), by path (--path), or interactively (no flags). The command now properly handles the agent lifecycle by ensuring the agent exists in the database before attempting to start its runtime, and it provides clear feedback at each step of the process.

# Risks

Low. This PR adds a new API endpoint for handling media attachments that doesn't modify any existing functionality. The only risk is potential resource consumption from large file uploads, which is mitigated by size limits and proper error handling.

# Background

## What does this PR do?

This PR implements a complete media API that allows:
- Uploading media attachments (images, documents, etc.)
- Retrieving media by ID
- Associating media with messages
- Retrieving all attachments for a specific message

The implementation uses both file system storage and runtime cache for redundancy, ensuring attachments remain available even if one storage method fails.

## What kind of change is this?

Features (non-breaking change which adds functionality)

# Documentation changes needed?

My changes include new documentation for the Media API in the form of a detailed README file in the routes directory.

8b:T1c11,# 🚀 ElizaOS CLI Publish Command Overhaul

## Overview

This PR introduces comprehensive improvements to the ElizaOS CLI publish command, transforming it from a GitHub-only publishing tool into a complete publishing solution that handles npm, GitHub, and registry publishing in a single command.

## Problem Statement

The current `elizaos publish` command had several critical limitations:

### 1. **Incomplete Publishing Workflow**
- Only published to GitHub, requiring manual npm publishing
- Users had to run separate commands for complete package distribution
- No unified workflow for plugin developers

### 2. **Registry Infrastructure Issues**
- Hardcoded registry URLs throughout codebase (`elizaos/registry` → `elizaos-plugins/registry`)
- Registry publishing created massive diffs by reordering entire index.json
- Incorrect package naming with `@elizaos-plugins/` prefix instead of actual npm names
- Environment loading issues preventing GitHub token access

### 3. **Poor Developer Experience**
- Fragmented publishing process
- Inconsistent documentation across templates and docs
- No guidance on continuous development workflows
- Recursion issues when npm scripts triggered ElizaOS commands

## Solution

This PR implements a complete publishing solution through 5 focused improvements:

### 🔧 **1. Code Cleanup** 
- Remove unnecessary parenthesis for better code readability
- Minor syntax improvements

### 🏗️ **2. Registry Infrastructure Consolidation**
- **Centralized constants**: Created `packages/cli/src/utils/registry/constants.ts`
- **Updated registry URL**: Changed from `elizaos/registry` to `elizaos-plugins/registry`
- **Single source of truth**: All registry references now use centralized `REGISTRY_URL`

**Files affected:**
- `packages/cli/src/utils/registry/constants.ts` (new)
- `packages/cli/src/commands/publish.ts`
- `packages/cli/src/utils/parse-registry.ts`
- `packages/client/src/hooks/use-plugins.ts`
- `.github/workflows/plugin-publish.yml`

### 🛠️ **3. Registry Publishing Logic Fixes**
- **Correct package naming**: Use actual npm package name instead of `@elizaos-plugins/` prefix
- **Minimal diffs**: Line-by-line insertion instead of full file reordering
- **Alphabetical positioning**: Clean registry updates with proper sorting
- **Consistent indentation**: Standardized to 4 spaces
- **Conflict detection**: Added registry conflict detection and handling

### 🚀 **4. Complete npm Integration**
- **Default behavior**: `elizaos publish` now publishes to npm + GitHub + registry
- **Recursion prevention**: Uses `--ignore-scripts` to prevent infinite loops
- **Flag restructure**: `--npm` now means "npm only" (skip GitHub and registry)
- **Environment fixes**: Added dotenv support to `parse-registry.ts` for GitHub token access
- **Authentication flow**: Integrated npm login requirements into publish workflow

**BREAKING CHANGE**: `elizaos publish` now publishes to npm by default

### 📚 **5. Comprehensive Documentation Updates**
- **Updated CLI README**: Reflects new npm + GitHub + registry default behavior
- **Enhanced publish.md**: Added continuous development workflows and testing guidance
- **Plugin starter templates**: Updated with complete publishing and development workflows
- **Success messaging**: Added guidance for future updates using standard npm/git workflows
- **Testing integration**: Emphasized testing throughout development lifecycle

## Key Features

### 🎯 **Unified Publishing Command**
```bash
# One command does everything (recommended)
elizaos publish

# npm only (for private packages)
elizaos publish --npm

# Skip registry submission
elizaos publish --skip-registry
```

### 🔄 **Proper Continuous Development**
After initial publish, developers use standard workflows:
```bash
npm version patch|minor|major  # Update version
npm publish                    # Publish to npm
git push origin main           # Update GitHub
git push --tags               # Push version tags
```

### 🧪 **Enhanced Testing Integration**
```bash
elizaos test                   # Run all tests
elizaos publish --test         # Test publish process
elizaos publish --dry-run      # Generate registry files locally
```

## Technical Implementation

### Registry URL Migration
- **Before**: `elizaos/registry` (hardcoded in multiple files)
- **After**: `elizaos-plugins/registry` (centralized constant)

### Publishing Flow
1. **Authentication**: npm login + GitHub token validation
2. **Building**: `npm run build`
3. **npm Publishing**: `npm publish --ignore-scripts` (prevents recursion)
4. **GitHub Publishing**: Repository creation/update + registry PR submission
5. **Success Guidance**: Instructions for future updates

### Environment Loading
- Added dotenv support to registry parsing
- Proper GitHub token access from `.env` files
- Improved error handling for authentication failures

## Testing

- ✅ New publish workflow tested with `--test` flag
- ✅ Registry publishing logic validated with `--dry-run`
- ✅ Documentation examples verified
- ✅ Recursion prevention confirmed

## Migration Guide

### For Plugin Developers
- **No action required** for existing plugins
- New plugins get improved workflow automatically
- Future updates should use standard npm/git commands

### For CLI Users
- `elizaos publish` now does complete publishing (npm + GitHub + registry)
- Use `elizaos publish --npm` for npm-only publishing
- Follow new continuous development workflows in documentation

Adds instrumentation support to LLM plugins for better observability and debugging.

# Risks

**Medium** — Changes involve instrumentation code across plugin logic. Potential risks include:
- Performance overhead if not handled properly.
- Incorrect spans/tags leading to confusing traces.
- Integration issues with existing telemetry systems.

# Background

## What does this PR do?

This PR adds OpenTelemetry-based instrumentation to the LLM plugin modules. It introduces tracing for key functions to capture span data like plugin execution time, plugin name, and error logs if any failures occur.

## What kind of change is this?

- Improvements (misc. changes to existing features)
- Features (non-breaking change which adds functionality)

# Documentation changes needed?

- Execute multiple LLM plugin calls (successful and failing).

/plugin-dpsn plugin. No specific issue or ticket is tied to this contribution; it introduces a standalone improvement to expand ElizaOS functionality for dynamic, data-driven responses.


# Background

## What does this PR do?

- Integrates the **@elizaos/plugin-dpsn** plugin into the ElizaOS agent runtime, enabling real-time data stream capabilities.
- Adds relevant code to initialize the `DpsnService`, allowing other parts of the application to subscribe to DPSN topics.
- **Enables ElizaOS agents to automatically consume real-time streams published over DPSN**, providing dynamic responses to external events (e.g., market updates, decentralized sensor data, etc.).
- **Allows users to leverage existing streams from the marketplace at [streams.dpsn.org](https://streams.dpsn.org/)**.

## What kind of change is this?

**Features (non-breaking change which adds functionality)**

This integration brings real-time data stream capabilities to ElizaOS agents, enhancing their ability to respond dynamically to external events. **Users can publish their own real-time streams over DPSN for automatic consumption by ElizaOS agents and tap into existing streams from the DPSN marketplace at [streams.dpsn.org](https://streams.dpsn.org/)**.

## Detailed testing steps

   - Add `DPSN_URL=betanet.dpsn.org` and `DPSN_WALLET_PVT_KEY=<your_private_key>` to your `.env`.


    ```bash
    bun run build
    ```


    ```bash
    bun test
    ```


> For any further queries please reachout to us on  📥 [DPSN Developers Telegram](https://t.me/dpsn_dev)94:T1159,### PR Summary: API Key Authentication and Connection Management Enhancements

This pull request introduces a range of improvements to the Eliza project aimed at enhancing API key authentication and connection management for both the server and client components. Key highlights include:

---

#### **Server-Side Changes**
1. **API Key Authentication Middleware**:
   - Added a new `apiKeyAuthMiddleware` in `packages/cli/src/server/authMiddleware.ts` to enforce API key authentication for requests.
   - Validates incoming requests based on the `X-API-KEY` header and the `ELIZA_SERVER_AUTH_TOKEN` environment variable.
   - Skips authentication if the environment variable is not set or for `OPTIONS` requests.

2. **Integration of Middleware**:
   - Integrated the `apiKeyAuthMiddleware` into the server application in `packages/cli/src/server/index.ts`.
   - The middleware is conditionally applied to `/api` routes if the `ELIZA_SERVER_AUTH_TOKEN` environment variable is configured.
   - Added logging to inform if the server authentication is enabled or disabled.

---

#### **Client-Side Changes**

<img width="464" alt="Screenshot 2025-05-01 at 5 07 26 PM" src="https://github.com/user-attachments/assets/4b3e6a1b-1047-46a1-8233-392a3e3a5f62" />

1. **API Key Dialog Component**:
   - Introduced `ApiKeyDialog` in `packages/client/src/components/api-key-dialog.tsx`.
   - Allows users to input and store their API key in the browser's local storage.
   - Includes validation and user feedback through a toast notification system.

2. **Connection Status and Error Handling**:
   - Refactored `ConnectionErrorBanner` and `ConnectionStatus` components to use a centralized `ConnectionContext` for managing connection states.
   - Enhanced error messaging to handle unauthorized states and guide users to troubleshoot issues related to API keys.

3. **New Context Providers**:
   - **AuthContext**: Added in `packages/client/src/context/AuthContext.tsx` to manage API key dialog actions.
   - **ConnectionContext**: Added in `packages/client/src/context/ConnectionContext.tsx` to centralize connection state management.
   - Both contexts are integrated into the client application.

4. **Integration with Existing Components**:
   - Updated components such as `AppSidebar`, `ConnectionErrorBanner`, and `ConnectionStatus` to leverage the new contexts for managing authentication and connection states.

5. **API Client Enhancements**:
   - Modified `packages/client/src/lib/api.ts` to automatically include the locally stored API key (`X-API-KEY`) in all API requests, if available.

---

#### **UX Improvements**
1. **Onboarding and Error Messaging**:
   - Improved handling of unauthorized states by automatically triggering the API key dialog when needed.
   - Enhanced visual distinction between unauthorized and general connection errors using contextual color schemes.

2. **User Feedback**:
   - Added notifications for connection restoration or loss, re-authentication, and API key updates.

---

### **Key Benefits**
- **Security**: Enforces API key-based authentication for server endpoints.
- **Resiliency**: Improved connection error handling and recovery for smoother user experience.
- **User Experience**: Simplifies API key setup and provides clear guidance for resolving connection issues.

---

This PR ensures that the Eliza server and client are better equipped to handle secure API interactions, providing a more robust and user-friendly experience for developers.

97:T1189,finished work on plugins, agent command is almost done but i need to debug tomorrow and also work on the set command.

### **Plugins Commands**

**elizaos plugins -h/--help**

Issue: Help display had non-standard formatting with -h and --help shown on separate lines. Command also displayed the banner and version information, which is not standard for help output.

Solution: Standardized help flag display with -h, --help on the same line and removed the banner display for a cleaner, more conventional help output.

**elizaos plugins add|install**

Issue: Installation failed in monorepo contexts due to dependency loop problems, particularly with workspace dependencies. The command couldn't properly distinguish between monorepo root and other projects.

Solution:
Added detection for monorepo context
Implemented workspace package detection
Created special handling for monorepo root using workspace:* references in package.json
Prevented direct npm install at root to avoid dependency loops
Implemented standard installation fallback for other projects in the monorepo
Added fallback mechanisms for when workspace installation fails

**elizaos plugins installed-plugins**

Status: Working correctly - lists plugins referenced as dependencies in package.json, providing correct implementation for portable monorepo design. Works as expected in both monorepo and standalone projects.

### **Agent Commands**

**elizaos agent -h/--help**

Status: Working correctly - displays helper text, options, and commands as expected.

**elizaos agent list|ls (-j/--json)**

Status: Working correctly - provides appropriate error messages when no agents are running and correct agent information when agents are active. JSON flag works properly.

**elizaos agent get|g (-n/--name, -j/--json, -o/--output)**

Issues:
--json flag incorrectly required a name parameter when it should work without one
When using both -n and --json, the command saved to a file instead of displaying terminal output
--output flag was non-functional

Solution:
Made --json display JSON output without saving to file
Made --output save to file without displaying on terminal
Removed name requirement when using --json to list all agents

**elizaos agent start (-n/--name, --path)**

Issues:
Confused creation and starting functions
Included unnecessary options
Failed with "Bad Request" errors when starting existing agents
Unclear command scope trying to both create and start agents
Complex error handling and response parsing
Inappropriate flags like --json and misplaced --remote-url option

Solution:
Clarified command scope to focus on starting existing agents
Removed outdated options from help text
Implemented a consistent two-step process: create agent if it doesn't exist, then start its runtime
Created uniform implementation across all command forms:
Positional argument (agent start agentname)
Name flag (agent start -n agentname)
Path flag (agent start --path character.json)
Added proper agent lifecycle handling, checking if agent exists before starting
Improved error feedback with helpful messages like:
"Agent '{name}' is already active!"
"There is no agent by the name '{name}', would you like to create one?"
These changes enhance usability by making the commands more intuitive and robust, following standard CLI conventions and providing clear feedback to users.
